title: "Same-sex union legislation"
---

# Same-sex union legislation



&#x20; Marriage open to same-sex couples

&#x20; Legislation or binding domestic court ruling establishing same-sex marriage, but marriage is not yet provided for

&#x20; Same-sex marriage recognized when performed in certain other jurisdictions, and accorded greater rights than local same-sex unions (if any)

&#x20; Civil unions or domestic partnerships

&#x20; Limited legal recognition

&#x20; Local certification without legal force

&#x20; Limited recognition of marriage performed in certain other jurisdictions (residency rights for spouses)

&#x20; Same-sex unions not legally recognized

[Same-sex marriage](92656.md) is legal in the following countries: [Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Andorra), [Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Argentina), [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Australia), [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Austria), [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Belgium), [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Brazil), [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Canada), [Chile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Chile), [Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Colombia), [Costa Rica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Costa_Rica), [Cuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Cuba), [Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Denmark), [Ecuador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Ecuador), [Estonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Estonia), [Finland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Finland), [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_France), [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Germany), [Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Greece),[^cite_note-Greece-1] [Iceland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Iceland), [Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_Republic_of_Ireland), [Liechtenstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Liechtenstein), [Luxembourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Luxembourg), [Malta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Malta), [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Mexico), the [Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_Netherlands),[^cite_note-Netherlands-2] [New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_New_Zealand),[^cite_note-New_Zealand-3] [Norway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Norway), [Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Portugal), [Slovenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Slovenia), [South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_South_Africa), [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Spain), [Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Sweden), [Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Switzerland), [Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Taiwan), [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Thailand), the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_United_Kingdom),[^cite_note-United_Kingdom-4] the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_United_States),[^cite_note-United_States-5] and [Uruguay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Uruguay).

Same-sex marriage is recognized, but not performed, in [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Israel) and the United States territory of [American Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_American_Samoa). Furthermore, same-sex marriages performed elsewhere in the Kingdom of the Netherlands are recognized in [Sint Maarten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Aruba,_Cura%C3%A7ao_and_Sint_Maarten). Whether [same-sex couples](19771642.md) should be allowed to [marry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marriage) has been and remains the topic of debate worldwide. 32 countries and four jurisdictions worldwide have passed [constitutional amendments](261586.md) that explicitly prohibit the legal recognition of same-sex marriage and sometimes other forms of legal unions as well. Sixteen countries and 34 jurisdictions worldwide have authorized [civil unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_union) or [unregistered cohabitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cohabitation) for same-sex couples as an alternative to marriage. The legal name of those unions as well as the number of rights that they provide can vary greatly.

## Legislative efforts to recognise same-sex unions

### National level

The first legislation of [unregistered cohabitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unregistered_cohabitation) was [introduced in the Netherlands in June 1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_Netherlands).[^cite_note-waald-6][^cite_note-IHRC-7] However, the first substantial recognition of same-sex couples did not occur before the introduction of a new legal form, that of [registered partnerships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_union), which was [first enacted in Denmark in June 1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Denmark).[^cite_note-8] Many countries have followed with similar legislation since then. Legislation of these forms of legal unions still occur with a variety of different names, even after the introduction of same sex marriage, although sometimes they are overwritten by subsequent legislation of same-sex marriage.

As of 2 October 2026, certain alternative form of legal recognition other than marriage has been allowed on a national level in [Bolivia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Bolivia), [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Croatia), [Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Cyprus), [Czechia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_the_Czech_Republic), [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Hungary), [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Italy), [Latvia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Latvia), [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Monaco), [Montenegro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Montenegro), and [San Marino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_San_Marino).

As of 2 October 2026, legislation to allow an alternative form of legal recognition of same-sex couples other than marriage is pending, or has passed at least one legislative house on a national level in [Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Kosovo), [Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Peru) and [Poland](1238328.md).

As of 2 October 2026, same-sex marriage has been legal (nationwide or in some parts) in [Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Andorra), [Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Argentina), [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Australia), As of 2 October 2026, legislation to allow same-sex marriage is pending or considered in at least one legislative house on a national level in [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Japan).

**Notes:**

### Sub-national level

See also: [Same-sex marriage legislation in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_legislation_in_the_United_States)

In countries with a [federal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation) system of governance, [states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federated_state) and [territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territory_\(administrative_division\)) often may grant recognition to same-sex couples in their own jurisdictions, although [unitary states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state) with a level of devolution, such as [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Spain) or the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_United_Kingdom), also offer such powers to local authorities. The first jurisdiction to enact unregistered cohabitation was [British Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_British_Columbia) in [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Canada) in July 1992. The first jurisdiction to legalize registered partnership was [Greenland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Greenland) as part of the [Kingdom of Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Denmark) in April 1996. Finally, the first sub-national jurisdiction to legalize same-sex marriage was the [Canadian](5042916.md) province of [Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Ontario) in June 2003.

As of 2 October 2026, there are 33 sub-national jurisdictions worldwide that have legalized some kind of alternative form of legal union of same-sex couples other than marriage. Those are:

- In [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Japan):&#x20;

  - 31 [prefectures of Japan](74517.md) (Aichi, Akita, Aomori, Fukui, Fukuoka, Fukushima, Gifu, Gunma, Hyōgo, Ibaraki, Kagawa, Mie, Nagano, Nara, Niigata, Okinawa, Osaka, Ōita, Saga, Shiga, Shimane, Shizuoka, Tochigi, Tokushima, Tokyo, Tottori, Toyama, Wakayama, Yamagata, Yamaguchi, and Yamanashi);

- Two British overseas territories ([Bermuda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Bermuda), [Cayman Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_the_Cayman_Islands)).

For the time being, 63 sub-national jurisdictions worldwide have legalized same-sex marriage in nations where same-sex marriage is not legal throughout the nation's territory. Those are:

- [Fifty-one Native American Tribal Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_tribal_nations_in_the_United_States), namely the [Ak-Chin Indian Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ak-Chin_Indian_Community), the [Bay Mills Indian Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_Mills_Indian_Community), the [Blackfeet Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackfoot_Confederacy), the [Blue Lake Rancheria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Lake_Rancheria), the [Central Council of the Tlingit and Haida Indian Tribes **Note:**

## Prohibition of same-sex unions

### Legislative efforts

Various countries have passed (or tried to pass) legislation to prevent same-sex marriage or repeal the existing laws (in some cases including criminalization or fines). Sometimes approving explicit bans or defining marriage as solely the union between a man and a woman, excluding all others.

### Sub-national level

See also: [Same-sex marriage legislation in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_legislation_in_the_United_States)

In countries with a [federal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation) system of governance, [states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federated_state) and [territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territory_\(administrative_division\)) often may forbid recognition to same-sex couples in their own jurisdictions.

Note: In India all States must record all marriages under federal laws but no federal law allows or recognizes same-sex unions of any kind. In addition, States may pass their own laws on same-sex unions if they wish.

### Constitutional efforts

Thirty-two countries have passed [constitutional amendments](261586.md) banning a variety of same-sex unions. These amendments have taken several forms. Some are limited to banning only marriage. Others ban marriage and prohibit legislation providing "similar rights".

#### National level

[Cuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Cuba) was the first country to prohibit same-sex marriage in February 1976.[^cite_note-844] [Paraguay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Paraguay), [Honduras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Honduras) and [Bolivia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Bolivia) are the only cases that not only marriage is prohibited but also de facto unions.[^cite_note-845] [Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Vietnam), [Cuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Cuba), and [Ecuador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Ecuador) repealed their same-sex marriage bans in November 2013, April 2019, and June 2019, respectively.

As of 2 October 2026, thirty-three countries prohibit same-sex marriage on a national level. Those are [Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Armenia), [Belarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Belarus), [Bolivia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Bolivia), [Bulgaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Bulgaria), [Burkina Faso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Burkina_Faso), [Burundi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Burundi), [Cambodia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Cambodia), the [Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_the_Central_African_Republic), [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Croatia), the [Democratic Republic of the **Notes:**

#### Sub-national level

The first jurisdiction to explicitly ban same-sex marriage in its [Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Alaska) was the U.S. state of [Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Alaska) in November 1998, however, same-sex marriage was [legalised in the state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Alaska) in October 2014. The first jurisdiction to enact such a ban was [Yucatán](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Mexico#Yucat%C3%A1n), in July 2009, but since August 2010 the state recognizes same-sex marriages performed in Mexico and in August 2021 local congress removed the heterosexual definition of marriage from the state's constitution.

| State/Territory | Country | Date | Ban on | Upper House | Lower house | Head of state | Final outcome |  |

| Yes | No | Yes | No |  |  |  |  |  |

| [Kosrae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosrae) Kosrae | [Federated States of Micronesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federated_States_of_Micronesia) [Federated States of Micronesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Micronesia) | January 2005 | Marriage[^cite_note-952] | —N/a | *Failed* | —N/a | No No |  |
| [Yucatán](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yucat%C3%A1n) Yucatán | [Mexico](3966054.md) [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Mexico) | July 2009 | Marriage[^cite_note-953] | —N/a | *Passed* | —N/a | Yes Yes[^cite_note-Yucatan-954] |  |
| [Baja California](199793.md) [Baja California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_Baja_California) | [Mexico](3966054.md) Mexico | May 2011 | Marriage[^cite_note-955][^cite_note-956] | —N/a | *Passed* | *Signed* | Yes Yes[^cite_note-BajaCalifornia-959] |  |

#### National level (Non Constitution)

Some countries have explicit bans on same sex marriage but it is not in the constitution but rather laid out in other laws.

| Country | Date | Info |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Azerbaijan](746.md) | Unknown | Article 2.3 of the Family Code reads: "Marriage is a voluntary union of a man and a woman registered in the relevant executive authority for the purpose family."[^cite_note-968] |
| [Bahamas](3451.md) | Unknown | The Bahamas Marriage Act defines marriage as only being possible between a man and a woman.[^cite_note-969] |
| [Bahrain](18933277.md) | Unknown | Under Islamic law, same sex marriage is illegal.[^cite_note-970] |
| [Bangladesh](3454.md) | Unknown | Islamic law prohibits same sex marriage.[^cite_note-971] |
| [Brunei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brunei) | Unknown | Same-sex marriage is prohibited by Sharia law, which is enforced in Brunei.[^cite_note-972] |
| [Egypt](8087628.md) | Unknown | Egypt has never accepted same-sex marriage, and anyone caught trying to perform a ceremony could be arrested.[^cite_note-973] |
| [Guinea-Bissau](12186.md) | Unknown | The Civil Code's Article 1577 states that "Marriage is the contract concluded between two people of different sexes who intend to legitimately form a family through a full communion of life."[^cite_note-974] |
| [Iraq](7515928.md) | Unknown | Article 3.1 of the Personal Status Law defines that "Marriage is a contract between a man and a woman who is lawfully permissible to him, the purpose of which is to establish a bond for a mutual life and procreate children."[^cite_note-975] |
| [Jordan](7515964.md) | Unknown | Article 5 of the Personal Status Law states that marriage is a contract between a man and a woman who are legally permitted to form a family.[^cite_note-976] |
| [Lebanon](17771.md) | Unknown | In Lebanon, only religious marriages are permitted. 15 separate family laws are in force in Lebanon and are administered by separate religious courts. The country does not recognize same-sex marriage or any other form of legal partnership for same-sex couples.[^cite_note-977] |
| [Malaysia](3607937.md) | Unknown | Islamic law prohibits same sex marriage.[^cite_note-978] |
| [Maldives](19117.md) | Unknown | Section 410 (a) (8) of Maldives penal code states: “A person commits an offense if two persons of the same sex enter into a marriage.”[^cite_note-979] |
| [Myanmar](19457.md) | Unknown | Myanmar does not recognize same-sex marriages or civil unions under its national laws. The country’s legal framework, influenced by colonial-era legislation and religious family laws, does not provide provisions for such unions. Specifically, the Special Marriage Act of 1872, which governs interfaith marriages, assumes heterosexual partnerships and does not explicitly permit same-sex marriages. Additionally, religious family laws applicable to Buddhists, Christians, Muslims, and Hindus in Myanmar do not recognize same-sex unions On March 2, 2014, Myo Min Htet and Tin Ko Ko held what is considered Myanmar’s first public same-sex wedding ceremony. Dressed in traditional Burmese attire, they celebrated their union in front of approximately 200 guests at a hotel in Yangon. Despite the ceremony’s cultural significance, it held no legal standing under Myanmar law.[^cite_note-980] |
| [Nicaragua](21362.md) | Unknown | Article 72 of the constitution states that marriage is between a man and a woman.[^cite_note-981] |
| [Oman](22316.md) | Unknown | Same-sex marriage is prohibited by Sharia law, which is enforced in Oman.[^cite_note-982] |
| [Saint Vincent and the Grenadines](27228.md) | Unknown | Same sex marriage is banned in Saint Vincent and the Grenadines.[^cite_note-983] |
| [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) | Unknown | Sharia law defines marriage as between a man and a woman, therefore banning same-sex marriage.[^cite_note-984] |
| [Sierra Leone](27308.md) | Unknown | [^cite_note-985] |
| [Turkmenistan](198149.md) | Unknown | The Family Code of Turkmenistan defines marriage as a voluntary equal union of a man and a woman.[^cite_note-986] |
| [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) | Unknown | Because each has different needs, marriage in the United Arab Emirates is defined as a union between a man and a woman.[^cite_note-987] |
| [Uzbekistan](31853.md) | Unknown | Chapter 14 of the Uzbek constitution declares marriage to be based on "traditional family values".[^cite_note-988] |
| [Vatican City](32408.md) | Unknown | Same-sex marriage is forbidden in Vatican City due to canon law.[^cite_note-989] |
| [Qatar](8238258.md) | 1915 | In Qatar, same-sex marriage is not permitted by law. The strict Muslim nation of Qatar forbids same-sex marriage and all other same-sex relationships. LGBTQ+ people face significant discrimination in the nation's laws and cultural standards, and homosexuality carries a jail sentence or fine. Because of this, LGBTQ+ people in Qatar are not granted the same rights and protections as heterosexual couples, and homosexual marriage is neither permitted nor permitted. |
| [Syria](7515849.md) | 1949 | Same sex marriage has been banned in the country since 1949.[^cite_note-990] |
| [Libya](17633.md) | 1953 | [^cite_note-991] |
| [Ghana](12067.md) | 1960 | [^cite_note-992] |
| [Guatemala](17238567.md) | 1963 | Article 78 of the Civil Code of Guatemala (1963) defines marriage as a social institution where a man and a woman are legally united.[^cite_note-993] |
| [Algeria](358.md) | 1966 | [^cite_note-994] |
| [Lesotho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesotho) | 1966 | The common law definition of marriage is “a union of one man with one woman, to the exclusion, while it lasts, of all others”[^cite_note-995] |
| [Cameroon](5447.md) | 1967 | [^cite_note-996] |
| [Cape Verde](18962637.md) | 1968 | Marriage, according to Article 1551 of the Civil Code, is the "voluntary union between two people of different sexes, in accordance with the law, who intend to form a family through a full communion of life." According to the Civil Code's Article 1587, "A marriage contracted by two people of the same sex is legally non-existent."[^cite_note-997] |
| [Afghanistan](737.md) | 1971 | Article 25 in Afghanistans Law on Marriage (1971) states: “The marriage certificate is a document of man and woman which proves the contract of marriage.”[^cite_note-998] |
| [Liberia](17791.md) | 1973 | Sub. §2.1. of Title 9 of the Domestic Relations Law defines marriage as “a civil contract between a male and female”.[^cite_note-999] |
| [Indonesia](14579.md) | 1974 | There is no legal recognition of same-sex unions in Indonesia. Article 1 of the Law No. 1 of the Year 1974 on Marriage states unequivocally that marriage is "a physical and spiritual bond between a man and a woman as husband and wife, having the purpose of establishing a happy and lasting family founded on the Belief in God Almighty".\[1] Moreover, Article 2 states that a marriage is only lawful if it is in accordance with the laws of the religions of the respective parties. Meanwhile, Indonesians who have entered into same-sex marriage abroad are not allowed to register their marriage in Indonesia due to Article 1 of the Marriage Act.\[2] Additionally, Article 34(1) of the Law No. 23 of the Year 2006 on Civil Administration obliges all marriages to be reported to the local authorities within 60 days after marriage, and the explanation of Article 34(1) states that "marriage" can only be performed by a man and a woman.[^cite_note-1000] |
| [Somalia](27358.md) | 1975 | According to the 1975 Marriage Law, a marriage is a contract with equal rights and responsibilities between a man and a woman with the goal of starting a family.[^cite_note-1001] |
| [Solomon Islands](265083.md) | 1978 | Banned since 1978.[^cite_note-1002] |
| [Barbados](3455.md) | 1979 | In Barbados, same-sex marriage is essentially prohibited by the 1979 Marriage Act, which defines a marriage as one between a man and a woman.[^cite_note-1003] |
| [Iran](14653.md) | 1979 | Definition of marriage used follows from the Islamic definition of a union between a man and women. The marriage is prohibited and same-sex sexual activity is punished as stated in the Penal code.[^cite_note-1004] |
| [Bhutan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhutan) | 1980 | [^cite_note-auto-714] |
| [Togo](30138.md) | 1980 | [^cite_note-1005] |
| [Mauritania](18778516.md) | 1983 | [^cite_note-1006] |
| [Kuwait](7515890.md) | 1984 | Article 1 of the Kuwaiti Personal Status Law (1984) defines marriage as a contract between a man and a woman who is legally available to him, for the purpose of cohabitation, matrimony and strengthening the power of the nation.[^cite_note-1007] |
| [Republic of the Congo](19599929.md) | 1984 | The Republic of Congo Family Code (Law No. 073-84): Section II. Marriage. Article 127. - Definition. "Marriage is the public act by which a man and a woman establish between them a legal and lasting union, the conditions of formation, effects and dissolution of which are determined by this Code."[^cite_note-1008] |
| [Philippines](23440.md) | 1987 | Marriage (explicit recognition of only heterosexual marriage)[^cite_note-pcw\.gov.ph-725] |
| [Guinea](12176.md) | 1988 | [^cite_note-1009] |
| [Marshall Islands](19147.md) | 1988 | The Marshall Islands government Act, which was published in 1988, set down guidelines for marriage, birth, and death registration. Marriage-related terms only applied to couples of different sexes (i.e., one man and one woman).[^cite_note-1010] |
| [Senegal](27278.md) | 1989 | According to Article 101 of the Senegalese Family Code, "An engagement is a solemn agreement by which a man and a woman promise marriage to each other." This makes marriage between a man and a woman clear.[^cite_note-1011] |
| [Laos](17752.md) | 1990 | The Family Law defines marriage to be a union between a man and a woman. Laos has not introduced any specific legislation around marriage equality for same-sex couples.[^cite_note-1012] |
| [North Korea](21255.md) | 1990 | Marriage in North Korea is defined as between a man and a woman. Article 8 of the Family Law of the Democratic People’s Republic of Korea states “Marriage may be done only between one man and one woman.”[^cite_note-1013] |
| [Sudan](27421.md) | 1991 | [^cite_note-1014] |
| [North Macedonia](23564616.md) | 1992 | Family Law from 1992, section 6: "Marriage is a legally regulated union of life between a man and a woman in which the interests of the spouses, the family and society are realized."[^cite_note-1015] |
| [Peru](170691.md) | 1993 | Article 234 of the Civil Code establishes that marriage is the union between a man and a woman. The Civil Code establishes that "homosexuality" is grounds for divorce, separation and annulment of marriage. The Constitution (1993) establishes that cohabitation is the stable union of a man and a woman (Article 5).[^cite_note-1016] |
| [Yemen](350939.md) | 1993 | Same sex marriage has been banned since 1993.[^cite_note-1017] |
| [Panama](22997.md) | 1994 | Article 57 of the 1972 constitution states that marriage is the legal basis of the family, and rests on the equality of rights of spouses (no mention of gender.) Article 58 of the 1972 constitution provides for the right to register as civil marriage the de facto union of persons of different sex after cohabitating for at least five consecutive years in conditions of single partnership and stability.[^cite_note-1018] Article 34 of the Family Code (1994) establishes that people of the same sex cannot marry each other. In 2018, the Inter-American Court of Human Rights ruled that the American convention on Human rights mandates the recognition of same-sex marriage, recommending that countries issue decrees until legislation was brought into place. In March 2023, after seven years of delay the court ruled that there is no positive right to same-sex marriage in Panama under the constitution, halting any potential change for the time being through the courts.[^cite_note-1019] |
| [Fiji](10707.md) | 2001 | The Fijian Marriage Act of 1968 was adjusted in 2002 to explicitly state that "marriage in this registry (or place) which is in law the voluntary union of one man and one woman to the exclusion of all others." Additionally, previous Fijian Prime Ministers have been opposed to same-sex marriage.[^cite_note-1020] |
| [Turkey](11125639.md) | 2001 | The Civil Code of Turkey does not recognize same sex marriage.[^cite_note-1021] |
| [Benin](3459.md) | 2002 | "Marriage may only be contracted between a man of at least eighteen years of age and a woman of at least eighteen years of age," according to Article 123 of the Persons and Family Code (2002).[^cite_note-1022] |
| [Albania](738.md) | 2003 | Article 7 of the 2003 Family Law states that "Marriage can be concluded between a man and a woman who are 18 years or older." In accordance with Article 163, "Cohabitation is a factual union between a man and a woman."[^cite_note-1023] |
| [Comoros](5403.md) | 2005 | Article 13 of the Family Code (2005) defines marriage as a legal union contract between a man and a woman. Article 77 establishes that a woman may request a divorce if her husband is homosexual.[^cite_note-1024] |
| [Gambia](65656.md) | 2005 | [^cite_note-1025] |
| [Madagascar](18964.md) | 2007 | Law No. 2007-022 on Marriage and Matrimonial Regimes, Article 2, declares: "Marriage between two persons of the same sex is prohibited, whether celebrated before the Civil Registrar or performed according to traditional ceremonies." "Gender identity" is mentioned in Article 39 as a reason why marriage is completely null and void.[^cite_note-1026] |
| [Zambia](34415.md) | 2007 | Zambia's Matrimonial Causes Act, which specifically ruled same-sex marriages null and void, went into effect in 2007. Zambia added a new provision to its constitution in 2010 that specifically prohibits same-sex marriage. The term "domestic relationship" is defined by the Anti-Gender-Based Violence Act (2011) as "a relationship, between a victim and a respondent in any of the following ways: (b) the victim cohabits with the respondent in a relationship in the nature of a marriage notwithstanding that they are not married, were not married to each other, or could not or cannot be married to each other." Those in same-sex relationships are included in this definition.[^cite_note-1027] |
| [Tajikistan](30108.md) | 2008 | Same-sex marriage is banned in Tajikistan.[^cite_note-1028] |
| [Ethiopia](187749.md) | 2009 | Ethiopia's federal law places a statutory ban on same-sex marriage.[^cite_note-1029] |
| [Romania](25445.md) | 2009 | Romania's so-called Civil Code (Law no. 287/2009) forbids same-sex marriage by law.[^cite_note-1030] |
| [Kazakhstan](16642.md) | 2010 | The Marriage and Family Code defines marriage as a union with equal rights between a man and a woman. It also states "The factual cohabitation of a man and a woman, as well as persons of the same sex shall not be recognized as the marriage (matrimony)." Article 11(1) states marriage shall not be allowed between persons of the same sex.[^cite_note-1031] |
| [East Timor](19159399.md) | 2011 | The Civil Code (Law No. 10/2011), states the following: Article 1467 (Notion of marriage): Marriage is the contract concluded between two people of different sex who want to start a family through a full communion of life, in accordance with the provisions of this Code. Article 1517 (Non-existent marriages): 1. It is legally non-existent: e) Marriage contracted by two people of the same sex.[^cite_note-1032] |
| [Chad](5488.md) | 2013 | Marriage is defined as the union of a man and a woman in Article 28 of Law 13-008 2013-05-10 PR on the Organization of Civil Status in the Republic of Chad.[^cite_note-1033] |
| [Nigeria](21383.md) | 2014 | [^cite_note-auto2-768] |
| [Eritrea](17238590.md) | 2015 | Eritrea effectively bans same-sex marriage. The country does not recognize same-sex unions in any legal form, and there are no provisions for civil partnerships or domestic partnerships for same-sex couples. Furthermore, Eritrea criminalizes same-sex sexual activity under Article 310 of its 2015 Penal Code, which applies to both men and women. This law carries a penalty of five to seven years’ imprisonment. The legal framework, coupled with the absence of anti-discrimination protections, creates a hostile environment for LGBTQ+ individuals.[^cite_note-1034][^cite_note-1035] |
| [Malawi](19088.md) | 2015 | Malawi's Marriage, Divorce, and Family Relations Law went into force in April 2015. Among other reasons, the law forbids gay marriages from being acknowledged at all. Additionally, it defines cohabitation and marriage as involving a man and a woman. By defining gender as the sex allocated at birth, the legislation also makes heterosexual partnerships between transgender and cisgender individuals of the opposite gender illegal.[^cite_note-1036] |
| [Vietnam](202354.md) | 2015 | Law on Marriage and Family (2015) states that marriage and cohabitation are between a man and a woman. Article 8(2) states "The State shall not recognize marriage between persons of the same sex."[^cite_note-Trung_ương-769] |
| [El Salvador](9356.md) | 2018 | Article 90 of the Family Code establishes that it is a cause for absolute nullity of marriage when the parties are of the same sex.[^cite_note-1037] |
| [Nauru](21302.md) | 2018 | There is no legal recognition of same-sex couples in Nauru. Section 49 of The Births Deaths and Marriages Registration Act (2017) states that "Marriage in the Republic shall be the voluntary union of one man and one woman." Section 70 states that "A marriage solemnized in a foreign country shall not be recognized as a marriage in the Republic if the marriage is (a) between a male and another male; (b) between a female and another female."[^cite_note-1038] |
| [Singapore](27318.md) | 2018 | Singaporean Prime Minister Lee Hsien Loong reaffirmed that marriage will continue to be "between a man and a woman" at the same time he declared that Section 377a of the criminal code would be abolished. On November 29 of the same year, Singapore further restricted homosexual marriage.[^cite_note-1039] |
| [Angola](701.md) | 2019 | Marriage is defined as the voluntary union of a man and a woman in Article 20 of the Family Code.[^cite_note-1040] |
| [Ivory Coast](57744.md) | 2019 | Same-sex marriage is essentially prohibited in Côte d'Ivoire by the 2019 Law on Marriage, which defines a marriage as a partnership between a man and a woman.[^cite_note-1041] |
| [Mozambique](19301.md) [Mozambique](19301.md) | 2019 | Marriage and domestic unions have been banned since 2019.[^cite_note-auto1-787] |
| [China](5405.md) | 2021 | The Civil Code, which came into effect on January 1, 2021, defines marriage as a union between a man and a woman, thereby excluding same-sex couples from legal marriage recognition.[^cite_note-20minutos.es-790][^cite_note-auto3-791] |
| [Vanuatu](32443.md) | 2024 | In 2024, the Vanuatu parliament passed an amendment to the Marriage Act, defining a marriage as being between a man and a woman, banning same-sex marriage.[^cite_note-1042] |

**Notes:**

Article 18: Marriage, being a union of a man and a woman, as well as the family, motherhood and parenthood, shall be placed under the protection and care of the Republic of Poland.\
    ["Dz.U. 1997 nr 78 poz. 483"](https://web.archive.org/web/20181226211610/http://prawo.sejm.gov.pl/isap.nsf/DocDetails.xsp?id=WDU19970780483). Archived from [the original](http://prawo.sejm.gov.pl/isap.nsf/DocDetails.xsp?id=WDU19970780483) on 2018-12-26. Retrieved 2017-11-25.

[^cite_note-862]: &#x20;*Judgment of the Supreme Court of 7 July 2004, II KK 176/04*, W dotychczasowym orzecznictwie Sądu Najwyższego, wypracowanym i ugruntowanym zarówno w okresie obowiązywania poprzedniego, jak i obecnego Kodeksu postępowania karnego, a także w doktrynie (por. wypowiedzi W. Woltera, A. Zolla, A. Wąska), pojęcie "wspólne pożycie" odnoszone jest wyłącznie do konkubinatu, a w szczególności do związku osób o różnej płci, odpowiadającego od strony faktycznej stosunkowi małżeństwa (którym w myśl art. 18 Konstytucji jest wyłącznie związek osób różnej płci). Tego rodzaju interpretację Sąd Najwyższy, orzekający w niniejszej sprawie, w pełni podziela i nie znajduje podstaw do uznania za przekonywujące tych wypowiedzi pojawiających się w piśmiennictwie, w których podejmowane są próby kwestionowania takiej interpretacji omawianego pojęcia i sprowadzania go wyłącznie do konkubinatu (M. Płachta, K. Łojewski, A.M. Liberkowski). Rozumiejąc bowiem dążenia do rozszerzającej interpretacji pojęcia "wspólne pożycie", użytego w art. 115 § 11 k.k., należy jednak wskazać na całkowity brak w tym względzie dostatecznie precyzyjnych kryteriów.

[^cite_note-863]: &#x20;["Judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 11 May 2005, K 18/04"](https://ipo.trybunal.gov.pl/ipo/view/sprawa.xhtml?sprawa=3826). Polska Konstytucja określa bowiem małżeństwo jako związek wyłącznie kobiety i mężczyzny. *A contrario* nie dopuszcza więc związków jednopłciowych. \[...] Małżeństwo (jako związek kobiety i mężczyzny) uzyskało w prawie krajowym RP odrębny status konstytucyjny zdeterminowany postanowieniami art. 18 Konstytucji. Zmiana tego statusu byłaby możliwa jedynie przy zachowaniu rygorów trybu zmiany Konstytucji, określonych w art. 235 tego aktu.

[^cite_note-864]: &#x20;["Judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 9 November 2010, SK 10/08"](https://ipo.trybunal.gov.pl/ipo/view/sprawa.xhtml?\&pokaz=dokumenty\&sygnatura=SK%2010/08). W doktrynie prawa konstytucyjnego wskazuje się nadto, że jedyny element normatywny, dający się odkodować z art. 18 Konstytucji, to ustalenie zasady heteroseksualności małżeństwa.

[^cite_note-865]: &#x20;["Judgment of the Supreme Administrative Court of Poland of 25 October 2016, II GSK 866/15"](http://www.orzeczenia-nsa.pl/wyrok/ii-gsk-866-15/sprawy_ubezpieczen_zdrowotnych_ochrona_zdrowia/33055d0/6.html?q=&_symbol=652&_haslo=Ochrona+zdrowia&_sad=Naczelny+S%C4%85d+Administracyjny&_skarzony=Prezes+Narodowego+Funduszu+Zdrowia&_okres=2016_10). Ustawa o świadczeniach zdrowotnych finansowanych ze środków publicznych nie wyjaśnia, co prawda, kto jest małżonkiem. Pojęcie to zostało jednak dostatecznie i jasno określone we wspomnianym art. 18 Konstytucji RP, w którym jest mowa o małżeństwie jako o związku kobiety i mężczyzny. W piśmiennictwie podkreśla się, że art. 18 Konstytucji ustala zasadę heteroseksualności małżeństwa, będącą nie tyle zasadą ustroju, co normą prawną, która zakazuje ustawodawcy zwykłemu nadawania charakteru małżeństwa związkom pomiędzy osobami jednej płci (vide: L. Garlicki Komentarz do art. 18 Konstytucji, s. 2-3 \[w:] Konstytucja Rzeczypospolitej Polskiej. Komentarz, Wydawnictwo Sejmowe, Warszawa 2003). Jest wobec tego oczywiste, że małżeństwem w świetle Konstytucji i co za tym idzie - w świetle polskiego prawa, może być i jest wyłącznie związek heteroseksualny, a więc w związku małżeńskim małżonkami nie mogą być osoby tej samej płci.

[^cite_note-866]: &#x20;["Judgment of the Supreme Administrative Court of Poland of 28 February 2018, II OSK 1112/16"](http://orzeczenia.nsa.gov.pl/doc/8AE25DE4B1). art. 18 Konstytucji RP, który definiuje małżeństwo jako związek kobiety i mężczyzny, a tym samym wynika z niego zasada nakazująca jako małżeństwo traktować w Polsce jedynie związek heteroseksualny.
